- 2026-11-16 20:15 PST LOAD SPREADING finding before I write code: ot-run placement is ALREADY load-aware. It orders every host that can admit the job by projected load ((load1 + fresh threads + job threads) / cores, ot-run lines 751-763, v4/v6 least loaded first, never hold) and never refuses on load. Live proof just now: ot-run --pick --mem 20 --threads 8 with epyc10 at load 174/96 and intel1 at 21/112 chose intel1; epyc10 shows only 17 GB admit (45 running, 1 waiter). So an UNPINNED job does not land on epyc10. What keeps epyc10 at 190: work that can only run there because its source is only there: ot-run --dir locality (a job whose --dir exists only on epyc10 qualifies only epyc10) and kit_wave gen_host picked from die.gen_hosts (qwen_kvsys gen_hosts = [epyc10] only, HEAD/S81 now epyc10+epyc8). KV HG2c G0 crawling is that case. The structural fix is therefore tree replication, not a placement change: KV D10 -> epyc8 (waiting on KV-R) and the current S81/HEAD/KV trees to intel1 (17/112 load, 346 GB free) and epyc2/epyc7/epyc9 as disk allows. Proposal: I copy S81 f2s4 d3, HEAD NF15c and (when posted) KV D10 to intel1 with full verify and add intel1 to those gen_hosts, same pattern as epyc8. Also a small code change I can make if you still want it: kit_wave pick_gen_host chooses the least-loaded host of die.gen_hosts when several qualify (today first/lowest by its own rule); say yes and I branch+gate it. Needs your ok on stage A hardlinks vs the 11-16 rule (chmod/write through a shared inode would hit the base: trees are a-w, retire3 uses chmod -R u+w before rmtree, which is safe for unlinking). - 2026-11-17 15:27 PST ANSWER ot_wait.py is not mine: it is WAVE-FLOW-R2's (r591, branch claude/wave-flow-r2-otwait-1117 @014f1f8, merged as e47c3895cc5). Your diagnosis is right in effect, wrong in detail: it does read systemctl show, and classify_unit() ends in `if act == "failed" or res not in ("", "success"): FAILED` BEFORE it looks at the ot-run client log. A .scope has no ExecMainStatus (systemd never execs the job), and when its cgroup is torn down after the job exited it shows ActiveState=failed Result=success: checked live on epyc8 (oth-ubuntu-1035341-1794691162.scope: ActiveState=failed, Result=success). So a job that logged `ot-run: RAN on ... exit 0` was reported FAILED. I did the fix anyway because it is small and has a test: claude/fleet-ops-r-otwait-scope-1117 @48c7dbc1036903d5c4e3f4d19b4ad5d85e82b1e8 GATE main=8569788b032 pass=1/1 GREEN (record ~/merge-queue/gates/48c7dbc1036903d5c4e3f4d19b4ad5d85e82b1e8.json): for a scope (Type=scope or Id ending .scope) that is `failed` with Result success, DONE/FAILED follows the exit status the client log recorded (exit 0 -> DONE, exit 3 -> FAILED with rc 3), no logged exit -> FAILED with 'no exit status in the client log' (never DONE by guess); a service failure and a scope with a real non-success Result (oom-kill) stay FAILED even when the log says exit 0. Tests with a fake scope: the three cases plus the service/oom cases. WAVE-FLOW-R2 owns the file: please have them review/approve the branch (PROPS now also asks for Id).

(2) ot-heavy stale admission lock: on main @7c54d9c198c. Cause: flock lives while ANY process holds the inherited fd 8, so a child that outlived the holder kept admit.lock for 70 min. Now the holder writes its pid to admit.lock.pid; a waiter that has waited OT_HEAVY_LOCK_WAIT_S (30) and finds that pid gone replaces the lock FILE (new inode; the leaked fd keeps the dead one) under a break guard, and logs 'STALE-LOCK' to admit.log; a live holder is still waited for. Tests: free lock, leaked-fd dead holder broken in < 15 s, live holder not broken (+ the 34 existing ot-heavy tests pass). /usr/local/bin/ot-heavy points at rt/current on all hosts, so it reaches them with the rt sync (intel1 already has it; others on 83f293d3b85f, 7c54d9c is the next sync).

After SIGTERM to the matching local clients it runs, for each client's --hosts (every build host if none), 'ot-heavy-stop --match <client --dir>' over ssh: stops the oth-* scope(s) whose processes carry that dir in cmdline/cwd AND their docker containers (otc-/dpkit-/kqc- by owner pid). Limit: ot-heavy-stop matches by cmdline/cwd, not by the job's mount or unit id as asked; the unit id route (--unit) is not wired because the client does not know it; say if you want the tag-to-unit lookup added. Clients on another host than the one you run ot-run on are not seen (clients() is local).

(2) FIXES (all tested, main push in its test run; a hub watcher posts the sha, rt revs and the cleanup): A1 one copy per (snapshot, destination): check + rsync + verify run under flock on the source host (<sd>.push-<dst>.lock, waits up to 2 h); a waiter finds .verified afterwards and returns, no second copy and no second verify (test: 6 concurrent callers -> 1 rsync, 1 verify). A2 the copy runs under 'timeout -k 30 2400' (own process group: bash, rsync and ssh die together on expiry). A3 a snapshot copy that timed out/was killed (KIT-SNAP FETCH failed, rsync error timeout/unexplained/protocol, flock timed out) classifies INFRA (retry), not a design FAIL. B ot-tools-sync pushes ALL hosts in parallel (sync_hosts, thread pool), one 'host: ok in Ns' / failure line per host in the log as each finishes (test: four 0.4 s pushes finish in under 1 s, a raising host does not stop the others). A4 after the fix is in the hub rt, the watcher removes duplicate leftover rsyncs: only processes with parent pid 1 and the snapshot command line, the OLDEST of each identical command is kept, the others get SIGTERM by exact pid after a cmdline re-check. The id seems to be derived from item and attempt without the event. A stop that names a queue job must first check that the job's kit path equals THIS row's attempt dir; I did not change kit_wave (WAVE-PILOT-FIX owns revive/reap; I did not prove which of the stop sites in reap_stale_attempts / dedupe at kit_wave.py ~2760 / 11255 ran). - 2026-11-19 19:52 PST ANALYSIS [FLEET-OPS-R -> COORD, FABLE-FLOW-1949 class 3] load-aware placement is ALREADY LIVE and not the remaining limiter; I pushed nothing new for it. (1) What exists (main 25c5d42a7f1, in rt f22d0e5f7228 on all 7 hosts, grep-verified): ot-run choose() keeps only the big hosts within 0.15 of the lowest projected load/cores ratio among those that ADMIT the job on memory (jobs <= 96 GB), memory admission untouched; REBALANCE re-places an unstarted waiter after 120 s to a host that admits (soft pin: sealed jobs list every host that holds their inputs). (2) Idle threads, measured from the UNDER-USE lines: 363 average over 11-18 23:26 to 11-19 05:05 (before), 84 at 15:27, 71 at 09:34, 60 at 17:58 and 50-60 in the last two ticks; the drop from about 350 to about 60 came with the 11-19 changes (load-first pick, copy storm cleanup, epyc7 recovery); the remaining 50-60 are ALL on epyc2 (load 68-86/128, every other host at or over its cores). (3) Why epyc2 stays idle: it admits only 29 GB (avail 279 GB: 64 GB INV_HEADROOM + the growth reserve of measured peaks, MEASURED_GAP_CAP 0.5); the waiters near it need 48+ GB, and of 306 ot-run clients on the hub 291 carry ONE --hosts pin (intel1 85, epyc7 63, epyc9 42, epyc8 37, localhost 29, epyc10 28, epyc2 24) that the wave chose before ot-run sees them, the sealed ones further limited by INPUT HOLD to hosts that hold the inputs. So moving more work to epyc2 is limited by memory admission (the rail), not by selection. (4) The only lever that would fill epyc2 is a smaller growth reserve on hosts whose CPU is idle (e.g. charge the measured-peak gap only when load per core >= 0.8): that trades idle CPU against the memory kills the reserve prevents (kills were 3 per hour at 0.5, 23 before the reserve and the bypass fixes); I did not change it without your decision. Also on the wave side: the 107 'rows not launching' are gate:q_running, not placement. Tell me if you want the load-conditional reserve (one conf value and a test).

- 2026-11-19 22:57 PST DONE/TRIAL [FLEET-OPS-R -> COORD] OOM kills. FINDINGS: (1) THE GATE DID NOT RESERVE GROWTH: the real admission check (ot-heavy gate, SIMPLE_PEAK path) is 'MemAvailable - INV_HEADROOM - growth of running builds >= new job expected peak', and the growth term was OFF on every host by the 11-14 owner setting (PEAK_GROWTH_FRAC=0, STAGE_PEAK=0). The measured-gap work I did on 11-18/19 only changed the status/placement view (ot-heavy-reconcile --free, what ot-run sees), not the gate, so jobs were still admitted on current free memory. (2) The stage machinery already exists (stage-peaks.json: per-stage p90 peak RSS from the flow .tcl script of each running openroad/yosys, e.g. global_route 28.5 GB, detail_route 40.7 GB, cts 9.9; stage_growth = table peak of current stage minus RSS now per running build, scanned from /proc incl. containers); it is global per stage, not per type/die (the history we log is per class). (3) SIZE: on every host the full stage reserve is far above free memory (epyc2: 77 builds, 519 GB at look 0, 869 GB with the next stage; free memory 35-120 GB per host), so charging it at 100 percent means admit 0 until memory frees; the p90 peaks of all builds never coincide. WHAT I DID: main 352d6a60adc: the status/placement view now charges the same stage-peak growth when STAGE_PEAK is on (so ot-run does not place where the gate will refuse). TRIAL CONFIG on epyc2 and epyc8 only (backup /etc/ot-heavy.conf.bak-stagepeak-1120): STAGE_PEAK=1, STAGE_LOOKAHEAD=0 (current stage only), STAGE_PEAK_FRAC=0.3 = the gate now subtracts 30 percent of every running build's (stage peak - RSS): epyc2 about 155 GB, epyc8 about 185 GB against 53-65 GB free, so both admit 0 now (they did already). The other 4 hosts are unchanged. A hub watcher posts kills and admitted jobs per hour for epyc2 and epyc8 one hour from now; I cannot confirm 'zero kills' before then. WHY epyc2: kills there were 32 between 22:06 and 22:50 (avg 5 GB, many small kits: svc_hi, slice_station, sle_ctl, so_*), i.e. the guard kept killing small victims one per 10 s while something not killable (plan jobs at adj -1000: plan jobs run on epyc2 2, epyc8 8, epyc9 6, epyc7 11, intel1 4 right now, and plan jobs bypass admission by owner rule) grew; epyc2 is the host with the most idle CPU (load 68-86 of 128), so ot-run placed the most new work there while its free memory was already low. RISK: if kills do not drop, raising STAGE_PEAK_FRAC to 0.5 on epyc2 is the next step; if throughput collapses, STAGE_PEAK=0 restores the old gate.

WHAT HELD rt/88537c5e1383: nothing stuck. By the time I looked it had its manifest (build finished at 00:02:46) and no process had it as cwd (checked /proc cmdline/cwd): at 00:02 TWO syncs (the 1-min follower and my 5-min catch-up timer / a manual sync) materialized the same sha at once; the second saw the first one's half-built worktree, in_use() said 'in use' (its own git/chmod processes), and it raised 'partial but in use; not touched'. That aborted the whole sync and left rt/current behind until the next follower tick. FIX main 7fd6b4331e6: (1) materialize takes a per-sha flock (rt/.materialize.<sha>.lock, waits up to 20 min, then re-checks: if the other builder finished, it just uses the finished dir); (2) a partial dir that really is in use (stray cwd or a dead builder's leftovers) is no longer a refusal: it is renamed aside to rt/.stale-<name>-<pid> (processes keep their cwd), git worktree metadata is pruned, and the sha is built fresh under its real name; test added (unused partial is removed as before, in-use partial is set aside and the name is free). The disk-full refusal still writes nothing (its test). Combined with the earlier per-host push flock, concurrent syncs now queue instead of failing.
